RESEARCH:
Of course\! You've correctly identified the symptoms, and your intuition that the animation logic is the source of the problem is spot on. The issue isn't specifically with the rotation button itself, but rather a combination of redundant state updates and conflicting animations that become apparent during rapid changes, like scrubbing the trimmer handles.

The "Invalid sample AnimatablePair" errors in your logs are the smoking gun. They indicate that SwiftUI's animation system is receiving conflicting or out-of-order instructions, causing it to fail and resulting in the jerky, out-of-sync behavior you're seeing.

-----

## \#\# Root Cause Analysis

The core problem is a conflict between two ways of telling SwiftUI to update the view:

1.  **Reactive Updates (`@Published`):** The `startTime`, `endTime`, and `showMinimumDurationWarning` properties in `TrimmerViewModel` are marked as `@Published`. SwiftUI automatically observes these and updates the view whenever they change. **This is the correct, modern approach.**
2.  **Manual Updates (`objectWillChange.send()`):** The `synchronizeStateAfterExternalChange()` function in `TrimmerViewModel` manually triggers a view update by calling `objectWillChange.send()`. This is redundant and fights with the automatic updates from `@Published`.

When you scrub the handles or apply a rotation, both mechanisms fire at once, leading to animation conflicts and the errors you see in the logs. The solution is to remove the manual/redundant update triggers and let the reactive properties handle everything.

-----

## \#\# Debugging and Solutions

Here are the exact lines of code to modify to resolve the issue.

### \#\#\# 1. Simplify State Synchronization in the ViewModel

The manual call to `objectWillChange.send()` is unnecessary and is a primary cause of the animation conflicts. By removing it, you let the `@Published` properties serve as the single source of truth for UI updates.

  * **File:** `TrimmerViewModel.swift`
  * **Action:** Comment out or delete the `objectWillChange.send()` line.

<!-- end list -->

```swift
// TrimmerViewModel.swift

    /// Synchronizes state after external changes (like rotation)
    private func synchronizeStateAfterExternalChange() {
        diagnosticLogger.logDebug("🔄 Synchronizing state after external change")

        // Force complete validation cycle
        forceStateValidation()

        // Additional synchronization for display consistency
        // objectWillChange.send() // 🛑 REMOVE THIS LINE
    }
```

### \#\#\# 2. Simplify Animations in the Time Code Component

Your `ReactiveTimeCodeComponent` has several explicit `.animation` modifiers that are fighting with each other and with the `.contentTransition` you've already set up. Simplifying this lets SwiftUI handle the numeric text transition smoothly without conflicts.

  * **File:** `ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift`
  * **Action:** Remove the two explicit animation modifiers. The `.contentTransition` is sufficient for a smooth update.

<!-- end list -->

```swift
// ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift

    @ViewBuilder
    private var timeDisplay: some View {
        let displayString = timeDisplayString

        Text(displayString)
            .font(font)
            .foregroundColor(textColor)
            .contentTransition(.numericText())
            // .animation(  // 🛑 REMOVE THIS MODIFIER
            //     .easeInOut(duration: animationDuration),
            //     value: displayString
            // )
    }
```

  * **Action:** Also remove the `onChange(of: time)` block. It contains a redundant animation trigger that isn't necessary because the view will update automatically when the `time` property changes.

<!-- end list -->

```swift
// ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift

    var body: some View {
        VStack(spacing: 4) {
            // ... view body ...
        }
        .onAppear {
            setupInitialAnimations()
            startTimeAnimation()
        }
        // .onChange(of: time) { _, newTime in // 🛑 REMOVE THIS ENTIRE BLOCK
        //     // Immediate update without debouncing for real-time responsiveness
        //     withAnimation(.easeInOut(duration: 0.1)) {
        //         // Trigger view update by accessing the time property
        //         _ = newTime
        //     }
        // }
        .onChange(of: isActive) { _, newActiveState in
            animateActiveStateChange(to: newActiveState)
        }
        // ... rest of the view ...
    }
```

### \#\#\# 3. Confirming the Minimum Duration Logic

Your logic for handling the 3-second minimum duration is already correctly implemented inside `TrimmerViewModel`. The `proposeTime` method calculates if the current duration is below the minimum and updates the `showMinimumDurationWarning` property, which correctly triggers the warning in your UI.

  * **File:** `TrimmerViewModel.swift`
  * **Code Reference (No Change Needed):**

<!-- end list -->

```swift
// TrimmerViewModel.swift

public func proposeTime(_ proposedTime: CMTime, for handle: TrimmerHandleType) {
    // ... validation logic ...

    // Show while duration <= minimum during drag
    let atOrBelowMin = (endTime - startTime) <= minimumDuration
    let wasBelowMin = showMinimumDurationWarning

    // Always update warning state to ensure consistency
    if wasBelowMin != atOrBelowMin {
        showMinimumDurationWarning = atOrBelowMin // ✅ This correctly updates the UI state
        // ... haptic and notification logic ...
    }
    // ...
}
```

By making these changes, you will establish a clear, single path for state updates, eliminate the animation conflicts, and achieve the smooth, synchronized timecode display you're looking for. ✨

SPEC:
Here is a spec-kit for an agentic software engineer to resolve the animation synchronization issues in the video trimmer.

-----

## \#\# Spec-Kit: Trimmer Animation Synchronization Fix

### **1. Objective**

Resolve the UI animation synchronization issue within the `FeatureRichTrimmerView` component. The primary goal is to ensure that the timecode labels (`startTime`, `endTime`, `duration`) update smoothly and accurately during all user interactions, particularly during rapid scrubbing of the trimmer handles. This will be achieved by eliminating the root cause of animation conflicts: redundant and conflicting state update mechanisms.

### **2. Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) & Success Criteria**

  * **Error Elimination:** The logs must be completely free of `Invalid sample AnimatablePair` errors during UI interaction with the trimmer.
  * **UI Smoothness:** All timecode labels must update without any visible stuttering, lagging, or flickering during fast or slow scrubbing of the start and end handles.
  * **Data Integrity:** The displayed `startTime`, `endTime`, and `duration` values must always accurately reflect the state within the `TrimmerViewModel`.
  * **Functional Parity:** The minimum duration warning logic must remain fully functional, displaying when the trimmed duration is less than or equal to 3.0 seconds.

### **3. Scope**

This task is strictly limited to refactoring the state management and animation logic within the following files:

  * **In Scope:**
      * `TrimmerViewModel.swift`
      * `ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift`
  * **Out of Scope:**
      * `VideoTransformBuilder.swift`
      * `AddMoveUnifiedState.swift`
      * Any business logic related to video export or Core Data persistence.

### **4. Action Plan & Implementation Checklist**

Follow these steps sequentially to implement the fix.

  * **[ ] Task 1: Simplify State Synchronization in ViewModel**

      * **File:** `TrimmerViewModel.swift`.
      * **Function:** `synchronizeStateAfterExternalChange()`.
      * **Action:** Locate and **delete** the manual `objectWillChange.send()` call. The `@Published` property wrappers already handle this reactivity automatically, and this line is the primary source of state conflicts.

    <!-- end list -->

    ```swift
    // TrimmerViewModel.swift

        private func synchronizeStateAfterExternalChange() {
            // ...
            // objectWillChange.send() // 🛑 DELETE THIS LINE
        }
    ```

  * **[ ] Task 2: Remove Conflicting Animations in UI Component**

      * **File:** `ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift`.
      * **Computed Property:** `timeDisplay`.
      * **Action:** Locate the `Text` view inside the `timeDisplay` property and **delete** the `.animation(...)` view modifier. The `.contentTransition(.numericText())` modifier is sufficient for smooth updates and does not conflict with rapid data changes.

    <!-- end list -->

    ```swift
    // ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift

        private var timeDisplay: some View {
            // ...
            Text(displayString)
                .font(font)
                .foregroundColor(textColor)
                .contentTransition(.numericText())
                // .animation( // 🛑 DELETE THIS MODIFIER
                //     .easeInOut(duration: animationDuration),
                //     value: displayString
                // )
        }
    ```

  * **[ ] Task 3: Eliminate Redundant `onChange` Animation Trigger**

      * **File:** `ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift`.
      * **Computed Property:** `body`.
      * **Action:** Locate and **delete** the entire `.onChange(of: time)` block. This block contains an unnecessary `withAnimation` call that contributes to the animation conflicts.

    <!-- end list -->

    ```swift
    // ReactiveTimeCodeComponent.swift

        var body: some View {
            // ...
            }
            .onAppear { /* ... */ }
            // .onChange(of: time) { _, newTime in // 🛑 DELETE THIS ENTIRE BLOCK
            //     withAnimation(.easeInOut(duration: 0.1)) {
            //         _ = newTime
            //     }
            // }
            .onChange(of: isActive) { /* ... */ }
            // ...
        }
    ```

### **5. Validation & Testing Checklist**

  * **[ ] Manual Test 1: Smooth Scrubbing**

      * Compile and run the application.
      * Load a video into the trimmer.
      * Drag the start and end handles slowly and quickly across the timeline.
      * **Expected Result:** The start, end, and duration timecodes update smoothly and accurately with no visual tearing or stuttering.

  * **[ ] Manual Test 2: Minimum Duration Warning**

      * Drag the start and end handles so the duration is less than 3.0 seconds.
      * **Expected Result:** The duration label correctly displays the warning state as defined in the UI logic.

  * **[ ] Manual Test 3: Rotation Interaction**

      * Tap the rotation button several times.
      * After each rotation, scrub the handles again.
      * **Expected Result:** The UI remains stable and responsive. Timecodes continue to update smoothly.

  * **[ ] Log Validation**

      * Open the console logs while performing all manual tests.
      * **Expected Result:** The logs contain **zero** instances of the `Invalid sample AnimatablePair` error message.

### **6. Expected Outcome**

Upon successful completion of this spec-kit, the video trimmer's timecode display will be fully synchronized with the underlying ViewModel state. All user interactions will result in fluid, immediate, and accurate UI updates without generating animation system errors in the logs. The user experience will be seamless and intuitive, reflecting a stable and robust implementation.
